1. Open-source components
==============================================================================

827 third-party components are distributed as part of this build. They are
grouped below by the licence text that applies to them; each group reproduces
that text once, in full, and lists every component it covers together with
that component's own copyright notice.

Where a component offered a choice of licences, exactly one was elected and
the election is recorded on the component's line. See the licence election
section for the policy.

2. Licence election
==============================================================================

An SPDX expression joined by OR is the licensor offering a CHOICE, not a set
of obligations that all apply. This notice elects exactly ONE branch of every
such choice and records it. An expression joined by AND is the opposite —
every conjunct binds, and all of them are reproduced.

Elections are made by the following preference order, most preferred first:

    MIT
    ISC
    BSD-2-Clause
    BSD-3-Clause
    0BSD
    MIT-0

    CC0-1.0
    MPL-2.0
    Unicode-3.0
    Unicode-DFS-2016
    CDLA-Permissive-2.0
    bzip2-1.0.6

No copyleft licence appears in that list. Where an upstream offered one as a
branch, the permissive branch was taken; the elections below are the complete
record.

==============================================================================
4. Bundled icons
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Lucide
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every client ships Lucide icon geometry. It is not consumed as a package on
the native clients: packages/icons reads the path data out of lucide-react and
emits it into per-platform sources — SVG assets, Compose ImageVectors, an
Xcode asset catalogue and a Rust registry — so the same paths are reproduced
in all four builds.

The desktop application additionally reaches gpui-component's own bundled set
of 99 Lucide-derived SVGs through the asset-source fallback in
apps/desktop/crates/app/src/assets.rs.

..............................................................................
ISC License — Lucide
..............................................................................

The SVG icons in this directory are from Lucide (https://lucide.dev),
copied so the desktop app's ExpIcon set is self-contained (masterplan-v3
§4.7 — several glyphs the app needs are not in gpui-component's bundled
icon set). File names match the Lucide glyph names one-to-one.

Exceptions — two sign-in brand marks (EXP-9), not Lucide glyphs:
- apple.svg  — Apple logo glyph (path from Simple Icons, CC0), rendered
  monochrome on the "Sign in with Apple" button.
- google.svg — the official multi-color Google "G" from Google's
  sign-in branding assets, rendered full-color on the "Sign in with
  Google" button. Both are used nominatively to label their providers'
  sign-in buttons.

ISC License

Copyright (c) for portions of Lucide are held by Cole Bemis 2013-2022 as
part of Feather (MIT). All other copyright (c) for Lucide are held by
Lucide Contributors 2022.

Permission to use, copy, modify, and/or distribute this software for any
purpose with or without fee is hereby granted, provided that the above
copyright notice and this permission notice appear in all copies.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS" AND THE AUTHOR DISCLAIMS ALL WARRANTIES
WITH REGARD TO THIS SOFTWARE INCLUDING ALL IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF
MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR BE LIABLE FOR
ANY SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN
ACTION OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF
OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

5. Bundled data
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Emojibase
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every client bundles an emoji dataset (labels, GitHub-style shortcodes, search
tags and skin-tone variants) for its emoji picker and :shortcode autocomplete.
It is not consumed as a package: packages/emoji projects the emojibase-data
package into one JSON file (apps/web/src/lib/emoji.generated.json,
apps/ios/Exponential/Resources/emoji.json,
apps/android/app/src/main/assets/emoji.json, apps/desktop/assets/emoji.json),
so the same data is reproduced in all four builds. The emoji themselves are
Unicode characters; only the descriptive data comes from Emojibase.

==============================================================================
6. Vendored source
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Vendored source in the desktop application
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Two crates in this repository contain third-party source that was copied in
and modified rather than depended on. Both are Apache-2.0, and both carry the
statement of changes that Apache-2.0 section 4(b) requires. Their NOTICE files
are reproduced in full below.

..............................................................................
apps/desktop/crates/ui/NOTICE
..............................................................................

ui — vendored third-party code (EXP-269)
========================================

The `ui` crate is Exponential's own source, covered by the repository's
Apache License 2.0 (see /LICENSE at the repo root) — EXCEPT the vendored
third-party files listed below. Those are also Apache License 2.0 (see the
LICENSE-APACHE-2.0 file in this directory), but their upstream authors keep
their copyright; this NOTICE records their provenance.

src/window_frame.rs
-------------------

Provenance

- Upstream: gpui-component `crates/ui/src/window_border.rs`
  (https://github.com/longbridge/gpui-component, rev
  a9a7341c35b62f27ff512371c62419342264710c — the same rev the desktop
  workspace pins in apps/desktop/Cargo.toml), Apache-2.0,
  Copyright the gpui-component authors (Longbridge and contributors).
- gpui-component's version is itself derived from the Zed editor's
  `window_shadow.rs` gpui example (https://github.com/zed-industries/zed),
  Apache-2.0, Copyright the Zed contributors.

Local modifications (Exponential, EXP-269)

- `FRAME_RADIUS` is 12px (the glass section radius). Upstream hardcodes
  `BORDER_RADIUS: px(0.0)` — carrying a copy is the only way to change it,
  since the constant is private and not exposed through any parameter.
- All four corners are rounded; the stock version rounds only the top pair.
- `window_paddings` is deliberately NOT copied. The public
  `gpui_component::window_paddings` stays the single source of truth for the
  client-inset math (it is radius-independent).
- `frame_radii` / `round_to_frame` are Exponential additions: gpui clips
  children with a RECTANGULAR content mask, so every layer that paints to the
  window edge has to apply the frame's radii itself.

src/title_bar.rs
----------------

Provenance

- Upstream: gpui-component `crates/ui/src/title_bar.rs`
  (https://github.com/longbridge/gpui-component, same rev
  a9a7341c35b62f27ff512371c62419342264710c), Apache-2.0,
  Copyright the gpui-component authors (Longbridge and contributors).

Local modifications (Exponential, EXP-269)

- The close button's hover/active fill rounds its top-right corner with
  `window_frame::frame_radii`, and the strip itself rounds both top corners.
  Upstream paints plain squares, which land in the notch outside the rounded
  window frame — the red close hover flashed a square corner back over the
  window. `ControlIcon` and `WindowControls` are private upstream and
  `TitleBar` always renders them, so there is no hook short of owning the file.
- `title_bar_options()` is deliberately NOT copied — it returns plain
  `TitlebarOptions` with no styling, so the public
  `gpui_component::TitleBar::title_bar_options()` stays the single source of
  truth for every `WindowOptions` site.
- `SmallVec` children became a plain `Vec` (avoids a `smallvec` dependency
  purely for this file).

..............................................................................
apps/desktop/crates/gpui-markdown-editor/NOTICE
..............................................................................

gpui-markdown-editor — vendored third-party code (EXP-261)
===========================================================

This crate is vendored source, licensed under the Apache License 2.0 (see the
LICENSE file in this directory). The upstream authors keep their copyright —
this NOTICE and the crate LICENSE are authoritative for it (the repository as
a whole is also Apache-2.0 since EXP-352).

Provenance
----------

- Upstream crate: https://github.com/yuWorm/gpui-markdeditor
  (crates/gpui-markdown-editor, commit a422e2c, 2026-07-15), Apache-2.0.
- gpui-markdeditor is itself an extraction of the editor engine of Velotype
  (https://github.com/manyougz/velotype), v0.7.0 / commit 050943e, Apache-2.0,
  Copyright the Velotype authors (manyougz and contributors). The engine files
  in the extraction are byte-identical to Velotype v0.7.0.

Local modifications (Exponential, EXP-261)
------------------------------------------

Cargo.toml
- `gpui` now resolves through the workspace to the zed git pin (rev 1d217ee)
  instead of the unrelated crates.io `gpui 0.2.2` package; `publish = false`.
- Removed dependencies: `ratex-parser`, `ratex-layout`, `ratex-svg`,
  `mermaid-rs-renderer`, `directories`, `base64`, `futures`, and all
  `tree-sitter*` grammar crates. Three `code-highlight*` feature names remain
  as empty shells so their `#[cfg(feature = ...)]` gates stay known; enabling
  them is unsupported.
- (EXP-302) The fourth shell, `html-native`, was removed outright rather than
  left declared: it gated a single `tree-sitter-html` validator call plus the
  tests that depended on it, so `--features html-native` named a build that
  could not compile. `src/components/markdown/html.rs` now carries a
  documented always-true `html_fragment_is_unverifiable` guard in its place,
  and asking cargo for the feature is an immediate error instead.

Feature excision (the GFM interchange contract has no LaTeX/Mermaid)
- src/components/latex/mod.rs: RaTeX SVG rendering + on-disk cache removed;
  pure parsing/sizing helpers retained; render entry points are unreachable
  bail!() stubs.
- src/components/mermaid/mod.rs: Mermaid SVG rendering + on-disk cache
  removed; pure fence parsing helpers retained; the render entry point is an
  unreachable bail!() stub.
- src/editor/document.rs: `is_display_math_start` always false ($$ blocks
  parse as plain paragraphs/raw markdown); ```mermaid fences parse as ordinary
  fenced code blocks.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: `parse_inline_math` always None ($…$ and
  \(…\) stay plain text).
- src/components/block/interactions.rs: removed the `$$`-then-Enter
  display-math block conversion.

Underline / superscript / subscript excision (no GFM representation — the
interchange contract intentionally has none of them)
- src/actions.rs: removed `UnderlineSelection` and its cmd-u/ctrl-u bindings.
- src/components/block/interactions.rs + render.rs + runtime/mod.rs: removed
  the underline action handler, its registration, and `InlineFormat::Underline`.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: `<u>`, `^…^` and single-`~` delimiters
  are no longer recognized (match_open_delimiter); `u`/`ins`/`sup`/`sub`
  inline-HTML tags no longer map to styles (stay literal text);
  `toggle_underline` removed; `^` and `<u>`/`</u>` are no longer escaped on
  serialization (no longer special).

Byte-parity alignment with the cross-client GFM contract (EXP-261 P2 — the
gate is `crates/ui/src/markdown/wysiwyg_parity.rs` over the shared
CONTRACT_FIXTURES corpus in `crates/ui/src/markdown/serialize.rs`)
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: literal-text escaping is now
  CONTEXT-SENSITIVE (`literal_char_needs_escape`) — `*`, `_`, `~`, `\`, and
  backticks are escaped only where they could actually open/close a construct
  on re-parse; the previous eager per-character escaping rewrote plain prose
  (`2 * 3` -> `2 \* 3`, `snake_case` -> `snake\_case`) and broke byte parity.
  Known deliberate residual: an intra-word `*` (`c*d`) still escapes (it IS
  emphasis-capable in GFM) — semantically identical everywhere, one-time byte
  churn.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: `_` emphasis now follows GFM's word rule
  (cannot open after / close before an alphanumeric), so `snake_case_name`
  stays plain text instead of becoming `snake*case*name`.

GFM table delimiter row (EXP-261 — data-corruption fix)
- src/components/markdown/table.rs (`parse_alignment_cell`): a delimiter cell
  now needs only ONE dash, per the GFM spec (`:?-+:?`). Upstream required
  three, so `| - |` and short aligned rows like `|:--|--:|` were not
  recognized as tables at all — every row degraded into a separate paragraph
  on import, destroying tables authored on the other clients. Serialization
  still normalizes the delimiter row to the `---` / `:---` / `:---:` / `---:`
  forms, so such a table converges after one save with its alignment intact.

Ordered-list start numbers (EXP-261)
- src/components/block/state.rs, src/editor/document.rs, src/editor/tree.rs,
  src/editor/tests.rs: the importer keeps an ordered-list item's literal
  marker number and the serializer seeds each numbered run from its head
  item's value, so a list starting at N round-trips starting at N (GFM list-
  start semantics) instead of renumbering from 1.

Backslash-escape parse/serialize symmetry (EXP-261 — data-corruption fix; the
desktop editor saves whenever re-serialized bytes differ from what it loaded,
so any parse/serialize asymmetry silently rewrote the other clients' content)
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs (`escaped_sequence_token_len`): the
  parse-side escape whitelist now accepts `\` before ANY ASCII punctuation
  (the CommonMark rule). The old whitelist (`\\ \* \_ \~ \[ \] \` \^`) left
  e.g. `1\.` / `\-` / `\#` / `\>` / `\<` with a literal backslash in the
  visible text, which the serializer then re-escaped — web-stored
  `1\. not a list` became `1\\. not a list`, and `\<tag\>` mutated on every
  save. One deliberate exception: `!\[` keeps its backslash VISIBLE, so an
  escaped-literal image (`!\[x\](y)`, meaning the literal text `![x](y)`)
  stays distinguishable from the raw inline-image markdown this engine
  preserves verbatim; `locate_inline_link` gained the matching
  `!`+`\`-predecessor rejection and `literal_char_needs_escape` the matching
  emit-bare exemptions, making `!\[x](y)` the byte-stable canonical form.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs (`literal_char_needs_escape`): the
  serialize side is now the exact inverse of the parser. New escape arms:
  `[` when the tail would re-parse as a link/footnote opener (via the REAL
  locators over `plain_char_tokens`; `[not a link]` without a target stays
  bare), `<` when the tail would re-parse as an autolink or a styled
  inline-HTML container (`<tag>` alone is an autolink in this engine;
  unstyled `<u>text</u>` stays bare), and block-start markers at fragment
  start — `#` (1-6 run + space/EOL), `-`/`+` (space/EOL, or an all-dash run
  >= 3), `>` (always), `<digits>.` / `<digits>)` (1-9 digits + space/EOL).
  Mid-line prose (`2 * 3`, `a - b`, `see #EXP-42`, `1 + 1 = 2 [not a link]`,
  `version 1.2`) stays untouched. Known accepted residuals: fragment start
  is the line-start proxy (a marker opening a heading's/table cell's/styled
  run's text over-escapes — renders identically everywhere), and unnecessary
  escapes from other serializers (`\]`, `\(`, `\!`, a superfluous `\>` after
  `\<tag`) converge to the bare form on first save (one-time byte churn,
  then a fixpoint).
- Tests: `*_exp261` additions in the inline test module (escaped block
  markers byte-stable, any-punctuation unescape, angle/link/image literal
  shapes reach fixpoints, plain-text spec escapes, no-over-escaping prose
  corpus) plus `escaped_block_markers_survive_document_round_trip_exp261`
  in src/editor/document.rs (block-level: escaped markers import as
  paragraphs and round-trip byte-identically).

Host integration seams (EXP-261 — additive API for the Exponential desktop)
- api.rs / editor/mod.rs / editor/render.rs: `MarkdownEditorOptions.embedded`
  (auto-height mode: host-owned scrolling, full-width rows, no row
  virtualization, no focus-steal on mount) and `focus_first_block()`.
- src/host.rs + environment.rs: `ImageSourceResolver` trait +
  `image_source_resolver` env field (authenticated attachment fetching and
  staged paste bytes — relative `/api/attachments/` URLs are not filesystem
  paths); `enable_image_resize` env flag.
- src/components/markdown/image.rs: `ImageResolvedSource` gained
  `Decoded`/`Pending`/`Failed`; `width_param_from_src` (`?w=` display-width
  URL param, cross-client contract); `ImageResizeDrag`.
- src/components/block/render.rs: image render honors resolver states + the
  `?w=` width; resize drag handle on standalone images (feature-flagged by
  the env; commits `BlockEvent::ImageResizeCommitted`).
- src/editor/window_state.rs: `rewrite_image_sources` (targeted `](src)`
  replacement across blocks — draft-upload resolution and resize commits
  without a document reimport).
- src/editor/context_menu.rs: right-click on a standalone image emits
  `MarkdownEditorEvent::ImageContextMenuRequested` for a HOST-rendered menu
  instead of the built-in insert menu; new events `ImageContextMenuRequested`
  and `ImageResized` (enum dropped its `Eq` derive for the pixel position).
- src/host.rs + environment.rs + block element/interactions + editor events:
  `ReferenceDecorator` seam — the host decorates `@email` / `#IDENT` tokens
  as pills (render-time only; serialization untouched), with Cmd/Ctrl+click
  surfacing `MarkdownEditorEvent::ReferenceClicked` (mirrors the link
  hit-test path).
- src/actions.rs: key context renamed "MarkdownEditor" ->
  "WysiwygMarkdownEditor" (the host's classic block editor already claims the
  original name; the two action sets must not shadow each other).
- src/editor/window_state.rs: caret accessors for host autocomplete —
  `focused_text_before_caret`, `replace_text_before_caret` (typed-edit path),
  `caret_viewport_bounds`.
- (EXP-551) src/editor/window_state.rs: `insert_text_at_caret` — inserts host
  text (the emoji picker's glyph) at the caret through the same typed-edit
  path, resolving the target through `format_target` and re-focusing it so a
  popover click, which has already blurred the block, still lands.
- (EXP-568) src/components/block/state.rs + src/components/block/render.rs +
  src/editor/events.rs: `BlockEvent::SelectionUiChanged` — upstream reported a
  selection to the host only when the HOST set one (`set_source_selection`), so
  a pointer drag or shift-arrow was invisible outside the engine. Block render
  now diffs `(selected_range, editor_selection_range)` against
  `last_reported_selection` and emits on a real move; the editor forwards it as
  the existing `MarkdownEditorEvent::SelectionChanged`. The diff guard is
  load-bearing — hosts repaint on the event, so an unguarded emit would spin.
- (EXP-568) src/editor/window_state.rs: `has_nonempty_selection` and
  `selection_viewport_bounds`, plus
  src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs `selection_bounds` — the anchor geometry
  for a host's selection-triggered floating toolbar. Unlike
  `caret_viewport_bounds` these answer `None` for a bare caret, and a
  cross-block sweep unions the per-block highlight rectangles.
- (EXP-568) src/api.rs + src/editor/window_state.rs: `FormatCommand::Paragraph`
  — an explicit demote-to-paragraph, so a "Text" entry can read as a
  single-select sibling of H1/H2/H3 instead of a toggle.
- src/theme.rs: `ThemeFonts { ui_family, mono_family }` (upstream hardcoded
  `.SystemUIFont`; code had no monospace); editor/render.rs + block
  element/render read the theme families.

Embedded-mode row width (EXP-261 — layout-corruption fix)
- src/editor/render.rs: embedded rows are laid out `w_full` instead of a fake
  `100_000px` width clamped by `max_w(relative(1.0))`. Taffy cannot resolve
  that percentage max against an indefinite parent during its intrinsic pass,
  so every paragraph was MEASURED unwrapped (one line tall) while paint wrapped
  it at the real width — each block painted over the rows below it. Regression
  test: src/editor/embedded_layout_tests.rs.

Standalone-image overlay controls (EXP-261 — web parity)
- src/components/block/render.rs: the hover controls moved off the full-width
  block onto a wrapper that HUGS the image, and became a `…` menu button at the
  top right plus a drag handle on EACH edge (`ImageResizeEdge`), mirroring the
  web node view. The width probe now measures the picture rather than the
  column, and dragging back out to the full column width commits `None`, which
  drops the `?w=` param so the markdown stays canonical and the image keeps
  re-flowing (`BlockEvent::ImageResizeCommitted.width` /
  `MarkdownEditorEvent::ImageResized.width` are `Option<f32>`). Deliberate
  deviation from web: the upper clamp is the column budget, not the image's
  natural width, which this engine never decodes.
- src/components/block/state.rs + src/editor/events.rs:
  `BlockEvent::ImageMenuRequested`, forwarded to the host as the existing
  `MarkdownEditorEvent::ImageContextMenuRequested` (the `…` button and
  right-click open the same host menu).
- src/environment.rs + src/components/block/interactions.rs:
  `enable_image_source_editing` (default true, preserving upstream behavior).
  With overlay controls present, clicking anywhere in an image block used to
  swap the picture for its raw `![](...)` markdown, which reads as the image
  vanishing; hosts that own the controls turn this off.

Host toolbar command surface (EXP-261 — additive API for the static toolbar)
- src/api.rs: `FormatCommand` (bold / italic / strikethrough / code, H1-H6,
  bullet / ordered / task list, quote, link, clear formatting) and
  `FormatState` (the toolbar's pressed-button state).
- src/editor/window_state.rs: `apply_format`, `format_state`, and the
  `format_target` fallback — pressing a toolbar button blurs the block, so the
  last ACTIVE block stands in and is re-focused afterwards; without it every
  button would no-op on the very click that blurred its target.
- src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs: `set_block_kind` (toggling — re-picking
  the active kind returns a paragraph), `clear_inline_formatting`,
  `inline_format_active`, `has_selection`, and `InlineFormat::Strikethrough`.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: `style_active` and `clear_styles`.

Web-parity toolbar semantics (EXP-261 — the buttons must behave like the web
`ToolbarActions`/`LinkControl`, not merely exist)
- src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs: `pending_inline_style` — the style ARMED
  at a collapsed caret, ProseMirror's "stored mark". Toggling bold with nothing
  selected has no text to restyle, so the style is parked (anchored to the
  caret offset) and applied to the next inserted text via
  `insertion_attributes_for_current_offset`; from there ordinary inheritance
  carries it. Any caret move drops it, so it can never revive when the caret
  revisits that offset. `inline_format_active` reports it, so the button lights
  up before anything is typed. Upstream simply no-opped.
- src/components/markdown/inline.rs: `link_run_at`, `link_target_at` and
  `set_link`; src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs: `link_command_range`,
  `link_at_caret`, `set_link`. Together these are `extendMarkRange('link')` —
  the link commands act on the selection, else on the whole link run under a
  bare caret, so a link can be retargeted or removed without selecting its
  text. `FormatCommand::Link` takes an `Option<String>` (`None` unlinks, which
  is what web's empty-URL apply does), and `FormatState.link` carries the
  target so the host can prefill its URL field and light the button.
- src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs: `clear_inline_formatting` also returns
  the block to a paragraph — web's "Clear formatting" is
  `unsetAllMarks().clearNodes()`, so a heading/list/quote goes too.
- src/environment.rs + src/components/block/runtime/projection.rs +
  runtime/mod.rs: `expand_focused_links` (default true, preserving upstream).
  A focused `[text](url)` used to expand into its markdown source so the
  target could be typed over, which reads as the link vanishing mid-edit; web
  keeps rendering it as a link and edits the target through the toolbar. With
  the flag off the inline projection leaves such runs alone. Reference links
  and autolinks still expand regardless: they preserve their source spelling
  verbatim, so hiding it would make them uneditable.
- Tests: `*_exp261` additions in src/components/block/runtime/tests.rs (armed
  style styles the next typed text, caret movement disarms it, arm+disarm
  leaves plain text, link set/retarget/remove from a bare caret, clear
  formatting resets the block kind).
- src/editor/events.rs: `insert_image_path` — the toolbar's file picker feeds
  the same `ImagePasteHandler` materialize + placement path as a paste.

gpui API drift fixes (crates.io gpui 0.2.2 snapshot -> zed rev 1d217ee)
- `.flex_grow()` -> `.flex_grow(1.)` (takes a factor at this rev).
- `ScrollHandle::max_offset()` returns `Point<Pixels>` -> `.height` became `.y`.
- `ShapedLine::paint` gained `TextAlign` + align-width params.
- `FocusHandle::focus(window)` -> `focus(window, cx)`.
- `ClipboardEntry::ExternalPaths` + `ImageFormat::{Ico,Pnm}` match arms added.

Pointer text selection (EXP-282 — drag and double/triple click did nothing)
- src/components/block/interactions.rs (`on_mouse_down`): the pointer
  selection session now starts on the click that ENTERS the block. gpui's
  `track_focus` registers its focus-transfer mouse-down listener before the
  element's own listeners and bubble dispatch runs them in REVERSE
  registration order, so the block's handler always saw `was_focused == false`
  on that click — it set `is_selecting = false` and the drag was a no-op,
  while a second click (already focused) worked. Focus is still requested
  through `BlockEvent::RequestFocus` so the editor keeps its bookkeeping, but
  the session opens unconditionally, mirroring the code-language field's
  mouse-down which had it right all along.
- src/components/block/interactions.rs + runtime/mod.rs: `click_count` is now
  read on mouse DOWN as well (it only opened footnotes/links on mouse up) —
  2 selects the word under the pointer (`select_word` / `word_range_at`: the
  text is split into alternating word / non-word runs from
  `unicode_word_indices`, with an offset on a word's trailing edge resolving
  to that word rather than the following space), >= 3 selects the whole hard
  line (`select_line`). A fourth click deliberately stays on the line instead
  of cycling to the block: the block IS the paragraph, so the two only differ
  for multi-line blocks, where re-selecting the same line is less surprising
  (gpui-component's `click_count >= 3` rule).
- src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs: `pointer_selection_extent` — the
  word/line range pinned by such a click, honored by `select_to`, which may
  push the passive endpoint outward but never shrink inside it (upstream had
  no equivalent; this is gpui-component's `selected_word_range`). It dies with
  the pointer session (`end_pointer_selection_session`, `on_mouse_up`) and on
  any plain caret move, so it can never leak into a later unrelated drag.
- src/editor/selection.rs (`on_editor_mouse_move`): a same-block drag used to
  hit an early return here. A block's own `on_mouse_move` is hitbox-gated by
  gpui, so once the pointer left the block's bounds — a row gap, the padding
  band, past the end of the text — nothing extended the selection any more.
  The move is now forwarded to the anchor block instead of being dropped;
  cross-block drags keep their existing path, and a drag that never left the
  block still fabricates no cross-block selection. (Chosen over
  gpui-component's window-level move/up handlers: the editor div already owns
  the cross-block drag state and the whole editor area, so relaxing it reuses
  that machinery rather than adding a second, competing one. Consequence: a
  drag that leaves the EDITOR entirely still stops extending.)

Whole-line caret placement (EXP-282 — clicking empty space snapped to offset 0)
- src/components/block/element.rs (`wrapped_line_for_y`): the y offset handed
  to the line layout is clamped into the line's own band. The last hard line
  returned it unclamped, and gpui's `LineLayout::_index_for_position` derives
  its wrapped-row index as `y / line_height` and answers `Err(0)` once that
  runs past the line's wrap boundaries — so ANY click below the last text row
  put the caret at the block start. `character_index_for_point`
  (block/input.rs) shares this helper and is fixed by the same clamp.
- src/components/block/runtime/mod.rs (`index_for_mouse_position`): dropped
  the `y < top -> 0` / `y > bottom -> len` short-circuits, which ignored x
  entirely. The block shell is taller than its text (`py(block_padding_y)`
  plus `min_h(block_min_height)` against ~18.5px lines), so those padding
  bands are a large part of every row; the click now maps its x onto the first
  or last wrapped row. This is what makes an EXP-271 blank line (a U+00A0
  paragraph a few pixels wide) clickable across its whole band.
- src/editor/selection.rs + src/editor/events.rs: `handle_gap_click_caret` —
  the general form of the EXP-277 image-gap click. Inter-block gaps are row
  MARGINS (`row.mt(block_gap)`), so they belong to no hitbox and used to
  swallow the click unless an image was adjacent; any such click now routes
  the caret to the nearest flanking block at the offset nearest its x and
  opens a selection session there, so a drag begun in a gap still selects.
  Deliberately no tree edit and no `mark_dirty` (the image path's precedent):
  an untyped click must never make the host think the document changed. The
  row margins were left as margins — moving `block_gap` into shell padding
  would have made the whole visual band hit-test, but it perturbs the
  embedded auto-height layout the `embedded_layout_tests` pin, for no gain
  over routing the gap click.
- Tests: `*_exp282` in src/components/block/element.rs (first-click drag
  selects; double-click selects the word; triple/fourth click selects the
  hard line; double-click-drag never shrinks inside the word and retires the
  extent on mouse-up; clicks above/below the text band follow x; a U+00A0
  blank line is clickable across its band), src/components/block/runtime/
  tests.rs (`word_range_at` word/gap runs and edges), and
  src/editor/selection.rs (gap click routes the caret to the nearest block
  without editing or dirtying; a drag outside the block's hitbox keeps
  extending its selection).

Tests
- Deleted the math/mermaid/underline/superscript/subscript behavior tests;
  added replacements asserting the excised syntax stays plain text and
  round-trips verbatim (`*_exp261` tests). Feature-dependent code-highlight
  tests are gated behind their (empty, unsupported) feature names. `test.md`
  fixture retained.
- (EXP-302) The html-native-gated tests were resolved rather than left gated:
  those covering the LIVE inline-HTML style path now build their `HtmlNode`
  input directly, the way `inline.rs::inline_html_style` does at runtime; those
  covering the unreachable semantic document/render path were deleted; and two
  replacements assert what the crate now actually does — every fragment
  classifies raw, and raw HTML imports as RawMarkdown and round-trips
  byte-identical. `test.md`'s `<details>` assertion tightened from
  "HtmlBlock or RawMarkdown" to exactly RawMarkdown.

(This list is kept current as further vendoring changes land; see git history
of this directory for the precise diffs.)

9. Components deliberately not included
==============================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GPL-licensed components patched out of this build
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The desktop application builds on gpui, which upstream Zed uses alongside two
GPL-licensed crates: ztracing and zlog. Neither is part of this build. They
are patched out and replaced by clean-room shims that reproduce only the API
names the call sites need, and zlog does not appear in apps/desktop/Cargo.lock
at all.

No GPL-licensed code is linked into any Exponential artifact. Where an
upstream offered a choice that included a copyleft licence — self_cell offers
"Apache-2.0 OR GPL-2.0-only" — the permissive branch was elected and is
recorded in the licence election section above.
